# Tie-break: default size and slice allocation for a frozen code-retrieval benchmark

Answer in under 350 words. Be decisive; give numbers, not ranges. **Prefer a recognised default over
a bespoke design** — the operator's instruction is "go with a default baseline, middle of the road
whenever possible."

## Situation
Local code-retrieval system (dense CodeRankEmbed + BM25/FTS5 + RRF + cross-encoder rerank). We must
build a FROZEN hand-labelled benchmark. Labelling is human time — expensive and not reversible.

Current 30-query set is **saturated**: dense scores R@3 = 1.000, MRR 0.9444, so it cannot rank the
arms (dense 0.9444 > hybrid 0.9222 > hybrid+rerank 0.9016 — all within noise). Root cause measured:
most queries are answerable from the filename alone (prefixing chunks with file paths moved MRR
0.80 -> 0.98).

An earlier plan said 250 questions across 9 slices; an agent then invented "60-80". Neither number
was derived from anything. We are replacing that with a defensible default.

## Corpus actually available
Python 1,464 files · JS 849 · TS 16 · **PHP 106** (2 WordPress repos: a child theme and a Woo plugin)
· HTML 317 · **TSX 0 · Blade 0 · Twig 0**.

## Questions — answer all four
1. **Size.** Is **50** the right default for a frozen IR test collection of this kind (the TREC
   per-track convention), or is there a better-recognised default? If you disagree with 50, name the
   specific number and the convention it comes from — not a preference.
2. **Slice allocation.** Given the corpus above, how should ~50 questions be split? Name the slices
   and the counts. Is per-language stratification even the right axis here, or should it be by
   query TYPE (name-carried vs behaviour-described vs cross-file vs no-answer)? Defend the choice.
3. **Dev split.** How many additional questions for tuning the no-answer threshold, and must they be
   disjoint from the frozen set?
4. **Growth rule.** State ONE measurable trigger for growing the set later, so it grows on evidence
   rather than vibes.

## Constraint that matters
The set's whole job is to DISCRIMINATE. A replacement that saturates again has failed. Say
explicitly how your allocation avoids re-saturating, given that filename-answerable queries are the
known failure mode.

# ROUTER.md

This file is the first entry point for an AI agent working in this repo: it tells you what to read, what to run, and which files are canonical.

## Role split

- `ROUTER.md` = startup order and canonical entry points
- `GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md` = the north star; what the repo's goals and design tradeoffs answer to
- `AGENTS.md` = behavioral rules, decision quality, reversibility, blast radius, proof
- `README.md` = human-facing repo/product overview
- `ROADMAP.md` = pointer ledger for this repo's own maintenance work
- `CHANGELOG.md` = the end-of-iteration running log (first-class PDDA artifact; governed by `PROJECT/PDDA.md`)
- `RELEASES.md` = forward-looking release-planning ledger (first-class PDDA artifact; governed by `PROJECT/PDDA.md`)
- `PROJECT/PDDA.md` = the canonical PDDA contract and automation rules
- `utils/pdda/PDDA-INSTALL.md` = the extraction/install manifest PDDA was installed from
- `utils/pdda/pdda.sh` = the unified runnable surface (dispatcher + every deterministic check + `run`)
- `utils/pdda/pdda-doc-ready.sh` = the opt-in LLM readiness review; `utils/pdda/pdda-lib.sh` = shared helpers

## Startup sequence

1. Read `ROUTER.md` to understand the repo's operating order and canonical files. -> expect one clear next file, not a repo-wide scavenger hunt.
2. Read `GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md` for the repo's north star. -> expect the goals and tradeoff lens that every design choice answers to.
3. Read `AGENTS.md` before making recommendations or edits. -> expect explicit assumptions, a reversibility read on consequential changes, and verified claims only.
4. Read `README.md` for the repo's purpose and baseline usage. -> expect a short explanation of what is canonical here.
5. If the task is about the PDDA contract or enforcement model, read `PROJECT/PDDA.md`. -> expect the source of truth for lifecycle, roadmap, changelog, and enforcement rules.
6. Read `ROADMAP.md` only for repo-local maintenance state. -> expect a pointer ledger, not a copied plan body from another repo.
7. Before reporting success on repo changes, run `utils/pdda/pdda.sh run` or the relevant single check (`utils/pdda/pdda.sh <check>`). -> expect deterministic findings first, then any LLM review.
8. If you are exploring an unknown system, proposing a new spike, or are blocked, search `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/` and `CHANGELOG.md` for past context first. -> expect to recover memory of past struggles, gotchas, or decisions.

## Canonical rules

- Do not put phase checklists, build steps, or deep execution notes in `ROADMAP.md`.
- Do not edit `utils/pdda/**` in this repo. PDDA's runtime is installed here, not authored here; a local edit is overwritten the next time the runtime is synced from the canonical PDDA repo. Fix it upstream instead.
- `PROJECT/PDDA-ACTIVITY.jsonl` is runtime output, not source. It starts fresh in this repo and is gitignored.
- Every active doc in `PROJECT/2-WORKING/` must be reflected by a pointer in `ROADMAP.md` — a one-line ledger entry that links it. A working doc that should not appear opts out with `roadmap_exempt: true` in its frontmatter. Enforced by `utils/pdda/pdda.sh roadmap-coverage`; governance lives in `PROJECT/PDDA.md` -> "ROADMAP.md contract".
- Every captured GitHub issue doc in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-*.md` must also be parked in `ROADMAP.md` as a one-line queue entry immediately at intake, then promoted or removed later. Enforced by `utils/pdda/pdda.sh roadmap-coverage`; governance lives in `PROJECT/PDDA.md` -> "GitHub issue intake" + "ROADMAP.md contract".
- The long-term canonical deterministic surface is `utils/pdda/pdda.sh`; do not add wrapper commands unless a real external integration forces them.
- Do not override deterministic PDDA findings with prose.
- Do not report a win you did not verify with the relevant script or test.
- Update `CHANGELOG.md` at the end of each iteration; its governance lives in `PROJECT/PDDA.md` — do not re-specify CHANGELOG rules in `AGENTS.md` or elsewhere.

## Command rails

For baseline verification and document hygiene:

```bash
utils/pdda/pdda.sh run
```

For targeted PDDA debugging, run a single check by name:

```bash
utils/pdda/pdda.sh frontmatter
utils/pdda/pdda.sh status-table
utils/pdda/pdda.sh hardcoded-paths
utils/pdda/pdda.sh roadmap
utils/pdda/pdda.sh roadmap-coverage
utils/pdda/pdda.sh changelog
utils/pdda/pdda.sh stale
utils/pdda/pdda.sh quad-concepts    # opt-in: a "## Quad Concepts" section of 1-4 bullets (lever: .pdda-quad / PDDA_QUAD)
utils/pdda/pdda.sh glance           # read-only roll-up: title + Quad Concepts for each PROJECT/2-WORKING doc
utils/pdda/pdda.sh issue-doc-sync   # flag GH-*.md docs drifted from their GitHub issue state (warn-only; gh-degrades to cache)
utils/pdda/pdda.sh releases    # validate RELEASES.md, the release-planning ledger (warn-only nudge)
utils/pdda/pdda.sh releases-current  # read-only roll-up: RELEASES.md entries whose Status isn't "Shipped"
utils/pdda/pdda.sh governance  # governance-doc cross-reference + doc/code drift (this file, AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, ...)
utils/pdda/pdda.sh gh-refresh  # refresh the cached GitHub issue-state file issue-doc-sync reads offline (needs gh)
utils/pdda/pdda.sh doc-ready   # LLM readiness review — set PDDA_LLM_BIN (codex/claude/agy) for recommendations, else it self-skips
utils/pdda/pdda.sh catchup     # LLM repo triage and ROUTER.md recommendations — opt-in like doc-ready
utils/pdda/pdda.sh help        # list every command
```

## Routing hints

- If the task is about document quality, active-doc lifecycle, roadmap sprawl, or automation policy, start in `PROJECT/PDDA.md`.
- If the task is about repo-local maintenance state, start in `ROADMAP.md`.
- If the task is about the changelog, provenance, or end-of-iteration logging, the governance is in `PROJECT/PDDA.md` (the "CHANGELOG.md — end-of-iteration record" contract).
- To re-run this startup sequence mid-session (task switch, resume, post-compact, context drift), invoke the `/pdda` skill (`.claude/skills/pdda/SKILL.md`) instead of re-reading by hand.

# Guiding Principles

The north star this repo's goals and implementation decisions answer to. When a design choice is
unclear, the option that better serves these principles wins.

## Purpose

XYZ Code Intelligence is a **local-first code retrieval system** for answering natural-language
questions about codebases. It is the planned successor to Ask-Self, combining dense and lexical
retrieval with reranking on the operator's machine while keeping the repository's work state
reliable for long-running human and AI-agent collaboration.

## Principles

1. **Docs are the runtime state, not a record of it.** The current `PROJECT/**` docs *are* the
   project's state. If reality and the docs disagree, that is the bug to fix.
2. **Resumable by a cold agent.** Every active doc must let an agent with zero prior context answer
   "what was just done, what's next" in seconds — that's why the status header is a contract.
3. **Deterministic where judgment isn't needed.** Scripts enforce the mechanical rules; the LLM
   reviewer only handles what regex can't. Never make an agent re-decide settled hygiene.
4. **One canonical place per fact.** `ROADMAP.md` points, project docs hold detail, `CHANGELOG.md`
   logs outcomes. No fact lives in two places where they can drift.
5. **A clear signal of what is live.** `PROJECT/2-WORKING` holds only truly active work, so "what
   should an agent pick up" is never ambiguous.
6. **Low-friction and portable.** The contract must be cheap to adopt (a one-command install) and
   cheap to obey, or agents will route around it.
7. **Default to the reversible action; require evidence for the expensive one.** When an item is
   stale, orphaned, or its dependency has left the repo, *close / park / archive is the default* —
   those are cheap and undoable. Keeping it open is what costs: it buys recurring triage attention on
   every future pass. So the burden of proof sits on **keeping**, not on closing. State a default and
   the one fact that would overturn it, rather than presenting symmetric options — "close as obsolete,
   or re-scope?" is how an item sits untouched for nine days.
8. **A finding that was reported is not a finding that was seen.** Detection is only half a check.
   If a run's closing line contradicts its own output, the signal is lost no matter how correct the
   detector was — so summary lines must reflect what was actually found. Corollary of #3: being
   deterministic about *detecting* is wasted if the *reporting* rounds it off.
9. **Local-first.** Ingest, embedding, retrieval, and reranking run on the operator's machine with
   no network call on the query path. A cloud API may appear only as an explicitly opt-in comparison
   arm, never as the primary lane.
10. **Permissively licensed models only.** Every model weight the pipeline loads is Apache-2.0 or
    MIT (or equivalent). Verify the licence on the model card before a model enters any run; a model
    whose licence is unstated or non-commercial is rejected, not deferred.

## How to apply

When adding a feature or making a tradeoff, ask: *does this make project state more resumable,
less ambiguous, and harder to drift for a long-running agent?* If not, reconsider.

When triaging existing work, ask: *what is the reversible move here, and what single fact would
argue against it?* Then say that, rather than listing options.
# Project-Driven Doc Automation (PDDA)

PDDA is the document operating layer for this repo. Its job is to keep project plans, bug-fix docs,
research notes, and roadmap pointers clean enough that an agent can pick up work with minimal drift
and enough structure that routine hygiene can be automated instead of re-decided every session.

The core idea is simple:

- deterministic scripts enforce the parts that should never require judgment
- an LLM reviewer flags structural or planning-quality gaps that are hard to express as regex alone
- `ROADMAP.md` stays a pointer/index, while project detail lives in the individual project docs

## Goals

- Keep `PROJECT/2-WORKING` limited to docs that are truly active.
- Ensure every active doc answers two questions at a glance: what was just completed, and what is next.
- Make phased plans automation-ready by requiring explicit QA gates.
- Prevent plan rot: stale files, missing next steps, hardcoded paths, and hidden scope drift.
- Give agents one repeatable contract for project docs, bug-fix docs, and experimental plans.

## Non-goals

- PDDA does not replace the project docs themselves.
- PDDA does not decide product strategy.
- PDDA does not auto-rewrite nuanced plan content without review.
- PDDA does not turn `ROADMAP.md` into a second execution plan.

## Canonical document model

PDDA assumes four lifecycle buckets:

- `PROJECT/1-INBOX`: new ideas, rough proposals, untriaged notes
- `PROJECT/2-WORKING`: active docs that should be updated as work progresses
- `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED`: completed docs with an outcome
- `PROJECT/4-MISC`: reference, stale, superseded, or abandoned docs

Within that model:

- `ROADMAP.md` is the index of current, completed, attempted, and deferred work
- project detail lives in the individual `PROJECT/**` documents
- a working doc is the canonical source of truth for that effort until it is completed, deferred, or superseded
- `blank.md` placeholders are scaffolding and should be ignored by PDDA checks

## Required contract for active docs

Every doc in `PROJECT/2-WORKING` should have:

1. YAML frontmatter with at least `title`, `status`, `created`, `updated`, `owner`, and `goal`
2. a near-top status table with the exact columns:

```md
## Status

| What was just completed | What's next |
|---|---|
| ... | ... |
```

3. clear phase or work sections if the doc is a plan
4. a table of contents (`## Table of contents`) listing each phase, if the plan is multi-phase — so a
   cold agent can see the full phase span and jump to the live one without scrolling the whole body
5. QA gates or acceptance criteria after each phase if the plan is multi-phase
6. for any discovery or spike phase, its findings written **back into this doc** before its QA gate can
   pass (see [Discovery & spike phases (Memory Injection)](#discovery--spike-phases-memory-injection))
7. repo-relative paths only; no hardcoded absolute local paths
8. before moving to `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED`, a `## Lessons Learned (For Future Agents)` section appended to capture quirks and gotchas

Recommended fields when relevant:

- `related`
- `context_tags` (e.g. `[auth, flaky-tests, build]`)
- `reviewed`
- `branch`
- `non_goals`
- `gh_issue`
- `effort`, `complexity`, `risk`, `phases` — triage ratings; **required for medium-large work** (see
  [Triage ratings for medium-large work](#triage-ratings-for-medium-large-work))

## Quad Concepts (opt-in)

An **opt-in** glance layer, **off by default**. The `## Status` table says *where* the work is; Quad
Concepts says *what* it is — a 5-second read of the core problems a plan tackles and how, so an operator
can see whether the real pain points are covered. (Distinct from `context_tags`: those are for search;
this is for glance.)

When enabled system-wide via the `.pdda-quad` lever (or the `PDDA_QUAD` env var — **orthogonal** to the
enforcement mode), tracked plan docs must carry a `## Quad Concepts` section of **1–4 bullets**,
conventionally right after `## Status`:

```md
## Quad Concepts
- <pain the doc addresses> → <how it addresses it>
```

- **Shape (deterministic):** 1–4 **top-level, non-empty** `-`/`*` bullets in the first `## Quad Concepts`
  section. `pain → fix` phrasing is the convention (nudged by the LLM readiness rubric), not a hard regex.
- **Scope:** `PROJECT/2-WORKING`, `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-*.md`, and `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED` (the last keeps a
  glanceable summary for cold-start recall). `PROJECT/4-MISC` is out.
- **Enable:** set `.pdda-quad` to `on` (or `PDDA_QUAD=1`). The enforcement mode still governs whether a
  missing/malformed section merely reports or blocks. **Opt a doc out** with `quad_exempt: true`.
- Enforced by `pdda.sh quad-concepts` (deterministic, structure-only) plus a warn-only readiness rubric.
- `pdda.sh glance` (read-only, always available) rolls up `title + Quad Concepts` across `2-WORKING` for
  a one-screen view of what the active portfolio is addressing.

## Triage ratings for medium-large work

So automation can pick *which* task to pursue without re-reading every plan, every newly recorded
**medium-large** task or project carries four triage fields in its frontmatter:

| Field | Range | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `effort` | integer `1`–`5` | how much work — `1` low, `5` highest |
| `complexity` | integer `1`–`5` | how intricate / how many moving parts — `1` low, `5` highest |
| `risk` | integer `1`–`5` | blast radius + uncertainty — `1` safe/contained, `5` one-way-door or unknown |
| `phases` | positive integer | total number of phases in the plan |

```yaml
effort: 2
complexity: 3
risk: 1
phases: 4
```

`risk` should track the repo's existing reversibility scale (`Easy / Costly / One-way door`,
`AGENTS.md` #3): `1`–`2` ≈ Easy, `3` ≈ Costly, `4`–`5` ≈ one-way door / high uncertainty. It is not a
parallel notion of danger — it is that scale expressed as a number.

**Scope.** Required for medium-large work (project plans, experiments, features, multi-phase efforts).
Genuinely small/trivial docs (a typo, a path repoint, a ≤2–3 line bug-fix — the same floor as the
issue-first SOP) do not need them. "Medium-large" is a judgment, so *presence* is enforced by the LLM
layer, not a regex (below).

### How to combine them — derive, don't store

There is deliberately **no stored composite "score" field.** A frozen aggregate would (a) drift from
the three numbers it came from, violating Principle #4 (*one canonical place per fact*), and (b) bake a
weighting choice into every doc that you then cannot re-tune without rewriting them. Compute the
selection signal **live, at selection time**, from the raw fields:

- **`risk` is a gate, not an addend.** A trivial-but-risky task (`effort 1`, `complexity 1`, `risk 5`)
  is easy to *do* but exactly what automation should not auto-pick — folding risk into a linear sum
  lets it slip through mid-ranked. Gate on it instead.
- **`effort` and `complexity` are correlated** (complex work is usually effortful), so summing them is
  a rough "size" proxy, not two independent signals — treat the sum as one ease axis, not two.

Reference selection rule (tune the thresholds per repo):

```text
eligible      = risk <= 2 AND not ratings_provisional   # safety gate; risk >= 4 => route to a human
ease          = effort + complexity       # 2..10, lower = easier
pick          = among eligible, lowest ease, then fewest phases as the tiebreak
```

`ratings_provisional: true` is an **eligibility gate, not just metadata.** Auto-drafted intake (e.g.
the `/idea` skill) ships best-guess ratings marked provisional; a rough `risk: 2` guess on a large
effort must **not** become auto-selectable on the strength of that guess. So a provisional doc is held
out of auto-selection until a human confirms the ratings and clears the flag — the same "route to a
human" posture as `risk >= 4`.

This keeps the raw ratings canonical and queryable while letting the "what's the easiest *safe* thing
to grab" logic live in one place that can evolve. (See the resolved `priority` note under
[Proposed extensions](#proposed-extensions-not-yet-locked).)

### How this is enforced

- **deterministic (values)** — `pdda.sh frontmatter` validates the fields **only when present**:
  `effort`/`complexity`/`risk` must be integers `1`–`5`, `phases` a positive integer. A present-but-bad
  value is unambiguous, so it `error`s. The script does **not** force presence — it cannot know whether
  a doc is "medium-large."
- **LLM (presence)** — `pdda-doc-ready.sh` flags a medium-large plan that is *missing* the triage
  ratings. Whether a doc is medium-large is a judgment, so it stays advisory/warn-capped like every
  other readiness finding.

## Why the two-column status header matters

The status table is the front door for both humans and automation.

- The left column is the last verified state change.
- The right column is the next action.
- If either is missing, an agent has to reconstruct state from the body, which is slow and error-prone.

PDDA therefore treats the exact header names as a contract, not a style preference. The header must be
exactly `What was just completed | What's next` — there is no alias/compatibility window. (One was
specced with a `2026-07-31` cutover, but a single-repo system controls its own docs: no doc here used
an old alias, so a dated, silently-changing branch guarded nothing and was removed 2026-06-22.)

## Discovery & spike phases (Memory Injection)

Discovery and spike phases exist to *learn* — reverse-engineer an existing system, probe an unknown,
prove or kill a risky approach before committing the plan to it. Their output is durable **memory**, and under
Principle #1 (*docs are the runtime state, not a record of it*) that knowledge is project state. If it
lives only in an agent's context or a throwaway scratch note, a cold agent resuming the plan cannot see
what was learned, why a path was chosen or abandoned, or what the spike actually proved — and the work
gets re-done.

Contract: **a phase tagged as discovery or spike must write its findings back into the originating plan
doc before its QA gate can pass.** This is active memory injection. Concretely, that phase's section (or a clearly linked sibling
section in the same doc) must capture:

- **what was investigated** — the system/area reverse-engineered or the question the spike asked

./README.md:3:A local-first code retrieval system for answering natural-language questions about codebases — the planned successor to [Ask-Self](https://github.com/Hypercart-Dev-Tools/ask-self).
./README.md:7:**Status:** early-stage / spike. The full retrieval pipeline described below is not yet built — see [Status](#status).
./README.md:14:2. **Dense lane** — code-native embedding model, candidate `nomic-ai/CodeRankEmbed` (768-dim, via `sentence-transformers`).
./README.md:19:Build plan is 6 phases: decision lock → chunking/retrieval skeleton → frozen benchmark → model bake-off → conditional fine-tuning → Ask-Self sunset/migration. Full detail and evidence (embedding model comparisons, fine-tuning datasets, the original Hyperagent research report) live in `PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/`.
./README.md:25:| CodeRankEmbed installed and validated; v0.5 canonical research/build doc synthesized from prior research | Phase 1: chunking + hybrid retrieval skeleton |
./README.md:32:- `.embed-tmp/scripts/embed_repos.py`, `query_repos.py` — working proof-of-concept embedding pipeline: batch-embeds a repo with CodeRankEmbed, with CPU/memory throttling, per-repo process isolation, and profiling. Sidecar embeddings already generated for several external repos under `.embed-tmp/<repo>/`.
./README.md:46:Model weights (`nomic-ai/CodeRankEmbed`) download on first use into the default HuggingFace cache. Full usage notes, throttling env vars, and memory-safety details are in `SOP.md`.
./README.md:55:model = SentenceTransformer("nomic-ai/CodeRankEmbed", trust_remote_code=True)

./relay-system/2026-08-28/gh5-quantization-plan-review.md:1:# RELAY · GH-5 int8 quantization benchmark plan review
./relay-system/2026-08-28/gh5-quantization-plan-review.md:47:- Definition of Done: Comprehensive, execution-ready GCP int8 quantization benchmarking and quality validation plan with CPU feature verification, 6-layer teardown safeguards, decoupled retrieval scoring, backend runtime thread pinning, and calibrated static quantization.
./relay-system/2026-08-28/gh5-quantization-plan-review.md:64:Basis: The benchmark plan is structurally thorough and has exceptional teardown safeguards, but requires 4 targeted changes before VM execution: decoupling score_retrieval.py from the Gemini API, ensuring crash-path error traps exfiltrate logs before self-termination, pinning thread runtime configs for ONNX/OpenVINO to ensure 4-vCPU parity, and including query samples in OpenVINO static calibration.
./relay-system/2026-08-28/gh5-quantization-plan-review.md:68:- [Should] Decouple retrieval quality scoring script from Google Gemini API dependency
./relay-system/2026-08-28/gh5-quantization-plan-review.md:69:  - Citation: `PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/GH-5-QUANTIZATION-BENCHMARK.md:179-180` ("Score with `.embed-tmp/eval/score_retrieval.py` against `.embed-tmp/eval/queries-LTVera-Pandas.json`.")
./relay-system/2026-08-28/gh5-quantization-plan-review.md:70:  - Problem: `.embed-tmp/eval/score_retrieval.py:7-8, 76-84` hardcodes an external call to `gemini-embedding-001` and requires `os.environ["GEMINI_KEY"]`. When scoring candidate quantized CodeRankEmbed models on the GCP VM (or offline) against the local fp32 baseline, `score_retrieval.py` will fail if `GEMINI_KEY` is missing or if two local index directories are passed without Gemini.
./relay-system/2026-08-28/gh5-quantization-plan-review.md:71:  - Concrete Fix: Update Phase 4 to specify a direct local-comparison mode (e.g. comparing the candidate quantized embedding directory directly against the baseline `.embed-tmp/LTVera-Pandas/` embeddings and queries) without requiring Gemini API calls or keys on the benchmark VM.

./relay-system/2026-08-28/gh5-quantization-plan-review.md:159:benchmark, and no arm or phase was added in response to the review.

./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/NL-to-Code Retrieval Datasets — Full Evidence Table.md:27:**PHP coverage: exists, but only in a handful of resources and almost never with retrieval-grade relevance labels.** PHP is a first-class language in [CodeSearchNet](https://huggingface.co/datasets/code-search-net/code_search_net) (one of 6 languages; [241,241 train / 12,982 valid / 14,014 test docstring pairs in the CodeXGLUE version](https://huggingface.co/datasets/google/code_x_glue_ct_code_to_text)), in [The Vault](https://huggingface.co/datasets/Fsoft-AIC/the-vault-function) (4,696,756 PHP pairs), in [CommitPack](https://huggingface.co/datasets/bigcode/commitpack) (2,555,419 PHP commit samples) and [CommitPackFT](https://huggingface.co/datasets/bigcode/commitpackft) (24,791), in [The Stack](https://huggingface.co/datasets/bigcode/the-stack), in [CommitChronicle](https://huggingface.co/datasets/JetBrains-Research/commit-chronicle), and — for issue↔code retrieval with qrels — in [CORE-Bench](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11864v1) (PHP among 11 languages at Levels 2–3) and [SWELocMulti](https://web3.arxiv.org/pdf/2512.20482) (206 PHP repos / 4,591 PRs / 16,608 training instances). PHP is **absent** from CoSQA/CoSQA+/AdvTest/WebQueryTest (Python-only), from [CoREB](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.04615v2), and from RepoBench-Python/RepoEval/SWE-bench Lite.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/NL-to-Code Retrieval Datasets — Full Evidence Table.md:29:**HTML/template coverage: largely a gap.** HTML is present only as raw pre-training text or commit diffs, not as labeled retrieval targets: [The Stack](https://huggingface.co/datasets/bigcode/the-stack) lists `html` among its 358 languages, [The Stack v2](https://huggingface.co/datasets/bigcode/the-stack-v2) mentions scraped HTML, [CommitPack](https://huggingface.co/datasets/bigcode/commitpack) has an `html` split (2,366,841 samples) plus `html+erb`, `html+php`, `html+django`, `html+eex` template splits, [CommitPackFT](https://huggingface.co/datasets/bigcode/commitpackft) has 20,214 HTML + 10,910 `html+erb` + 399 `html+django` + 150 `html+php` samples, and [code-rag-bench/github-repos](https://huggingface.co/datasets/code-rag-bench/github-repos) contains HTML documents in its retrieval pool. The only benchmark-with-qrels HTML signal found is [CodeFeedback-ST inside CoIR](https://arxiv.org/html/2407.02883v3), whose language list includes HTML. HTML is explicitly *not* in the language lists of [CORE-Bench](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11864v1), [SWELocMulti](https://web3.arxiv.org/pdf/2512.20482), [The Vault](https://huggingface.co/datasets/Fsoft-AIC/the-vault-function), or [CodeSearchNet](https://huggingface.co/datasets/code-search-net/code_search_net). TypeScript is a second, smaller gap: absent from CodeSearchNet/The Vault/CoIR language lists, present in [The Stack](https://huggingface.co/datasets/bigcode/the-stack), [CommitPack](https://huggingface.co/datasets/bigcode/commitpack), [CommitChronicle](https://huggingface.co/datasets/JetBrains-Research/commit-chronicle), [CORE-Bench](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11864v1) and [SWELocMulti](https://web3.arxiv.org/pdf/2512.20482).

./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/NL-to-Code Retrieval Datasets — Full Evidence Table.md:67:| 27 | CORE-Bench (2026) | [arXiv 2606.11864v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11864v1) | 3 levels: L1 re-packages existing retrieval datasets; L2 built from SWE-bench-family PRs (LLM-filtered for answer leakage, repo checked out at pre-PR commit, AST/LangChain chunking, patch-aligned qrels); L3 adds agent-trajectory-derived broader context with a 3-vote relevance ensemble ([paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11864v1)) | 11 languages at L2/L3: **Python, Go, JavaScript, Rust, TypeScript, Java, C++, C, Ruby, PHP, Swift**; **HTML not listed** ([paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11864v1)) | query↔code (L1: text→code, code→text, code→code, hybrid), **issue→edited chunk (L2)**, **issue→broader context incl. docs/tests/config (L3)**; plus rewritten-query variants ([paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11864v1)) | L1 172,961 queries / 2.41M corpus / 823,345 qrels; L2 5,061 queries / 9.38M chunks / 52,712 qrels (632 repos); L3 2,580 queries / 2.61M chunks / 106,479 qrels (97 repos) ([paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11864v1)) | Three levels + original/rewrite query splits; separate SFT training split (628 repos, 53,301 queries, 1,218,875 qrels, 29.17M chunks) ([paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11864v1)) | **Yes — the most explicit qrels of any resource here** (multi-positive: 10.42 rel/query at L2, 41.27 at L3) ([paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11864v1)) | Retrieval metrics reported per level and per language (values in paper tables) ([paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11864v1)) | n.a. ./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/NL-to-Code Retrieval Datasets — Full Evidence Table.md:77:### Best 3–5 for **training** NL→code retrieval
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/NL-to-Code Retrieval Datasets — Full Evidence Table.md:80:2. **CoRNStack (`nomic-ai/cornstack-*`)** — [ready-made `<query, positive, negative>` triplets built from deduplicated Stack v2 with dual-consistency filtering and curriculum hard negatives](https://huggingface.co/datasets/nomic-ai/cornstack-python-v1); [ICLR 2025, trained the CodeRankEmbed retriever](https://openreview.net/forum?id=iyJOUELYir). The hard negatives matter more than raw pair count for retriever quality.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/NL-to-Code Retrieval Datasets — Full Evidence Table.md:90:2. **CORE-Bench** (if you can accept a 2026-fresh benchmark) — [180K+ queries with 823,345 / 52,712 / 106,479 qrels across three levels, multi-positive (10.4–41.3 relevant docs per query), 11 languages including PHP, TypeScript and JavaScript, plus a training split on repos disjoint from the SWE-bench family](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11864v1). It is the only fetched resource that evaluates *broader-context* retrieval (docs, tests, config, call sites), which is exactly the codebase-RAG setting.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/NL-to-Code Retrieval Datasets — Full Evidence Table.md:91:3. **CodeRAG-Bench** — [BEIR-format `qrel/test.tsv` with annotated ground-truth documents, plus execution-based end-to-end scoring and reported nDCG/MRR/Recall/Pass@1](https://github.com/code-rag-bench/code-rag-bench). Use it when you need to prove retrieval improvements translate into working code, not just better ranking.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/NL-to-Code Retrieval Datasets — Full Evidence Table.md:93:For issue-driven retrieval specifically, add **Loc-Bench_V1** ([560 test examples spanning bug/feature/performance/security issues with function-level gold edits](https://huggingface.co/datasets/czlll/Loc-Bench_V1/blob/main/README.md)) and, for a contamination-resistant sanity check, **CoREB** ([counterfactually rewritten LiveCodeBench problems with 23,827 graded qrels](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.04615v2)).
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/NL-to-Code Retrieval Datasets — Full Evidence Table.md:97:- **HTML/templates:** no labeled retrieval benchmark exists in anything fetched. Only [CoIR's CodeFeedback-ST language list](https://arxiv.org/html/2407.02883v3) includes HTML with qrels. If HTML/template retrieval matters, you must build your own eval set — the most viable raw material is [CommitPack's html / html+erb / html+django / html+php splits](https://huggingface.co/datasets/bigcode/commitpack), [The Stack's `html`](https://huggingface.co/datasets/bigcode/the-stack), and [code-rag-bench/github-repos HTML documents](https://huggingface.co/datasets/code-rag-bench/github-repos).
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/NL-to-Code Retrieval Datasets — Full Evidence Table.md:100:- **Leakage hygiene:** before trusting any headline number, dedup your training data against the eval corpora at function level — the published effect size is [up to 100% metric inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/1812.06469) and [demonstrated inter-dataset leakage from CodeSearchNet](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.07930). Prefer benchmarks with documented controls: [RepoBench (Stack v2 dedup)](https://huggingface.co/datasets/tianyang/repobench_python_v1.1), [SweLoc (benchmark-repo exclusion)](https://arxiv.org/html/2505.07849v2), [CORE-Bench (disjoint training repos, answer-leak filtering)](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11864v1), [CoREB (counterfactual rewriting)](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.04615v2).

./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:19:**Prepared:** 2026-08-28 · **Audience:** Noel Saw (Neochrome) · **Goal:** decide whether to fine-tune `BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5` locally for semantic retrieval over private repos (Python, JS/TS, PHP, HTML/templates, Markdown/tests/config/routes/API schemas), or adopt a better starting point.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:29:3. **Benchmark before you train.** Your control group already includes BM25 and hybrid+rerank. Add NightOwl-35M and CodeRankEmbed to that group. Most "fine-tuning needed" conclusions in this space are artifacts of benchmark leakage ([Allamanis 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1812.06469), [Hernández López 2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.07930)) — establish your real baseline first.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:30:4. **HTML/templates/config/schemas are a genuine gap** for every code embedding model surveyed. Plan a separate lexical (BM25) lane for those file types, and add [`jinaai/jina-embeddings-v2-base-code`](https://huggingface.co/jinaai/jina-embeddings-v2-base-code) as the best dense model to *test* for them (it is the only one whose declared language list includes HTML/CSS/Markdown/Shell/Dockerfile) — but note this is a coverage claim, not evidence that Blade/Twig/Jinja/server-rendered template retrieval actually works; validate it on your own template slice before relying on it.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:41:| [Matthieufromparis/bge-small-code-search-v1](https://huggingface.co/Matthieufromparis/bge-small-code-search-v1) | Trained on only 6,000 CodeSearchNet-Python pairs; reports NDCG@10 0.9761→0.9849 on 500 self-built held-out pairs. A 0.976 base score is implausibly high for real code search — a saturated, single-language, self-run eval, not a benchmark. |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:44:**BAAI never shipped a small BGE code model.** `BAAI/bge-code-v1` is a 1.54B-param `Qwen2Model` (Qwen2.5-Coder class), not a BGE-small derivative ([config.json](https://huggingface.co/BAAI/bge-code-v1/resolve/main/config.json)). The only BGE-derived code fine-tune reporting a recognized benchmark is [jamie8johnson/bge-large-v1.5-code-search](https://huggingface.co/jamie8johnson/bge-large-v1.5-code-search) (LoRA, Apache-2.0) at CoIR 57.5 vs 55.7 base — but single-author, self-run evals, no paper, and it is BGE-large, not small.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:50:## 3. Shortlist — candidates to benchmark against your control group

Needs `trust_remote_code` | **High** |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:60:| S5 | **Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B** (Apache-2.0 stand-in for non-commercial jina-code-0.5b) | [card](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B) | Qwen3ForCausalLM from Qwen3-0.6B-Base, 28 layers, instruction-aware, MRL | 596M | up to 1,024 (32–1024 selectable) | 32,768 | Multilingual; used as the hard-neg miner by NightOwl | n.a. on card (discloses no training data/objective) | Instruction-tuned contrastive | n.a. | Third-party MTEB-Code 74.69% (vs 78.72% for jina-code-0.5b); MTEB retrieval 64.64 | Apache-2.0 | 2026-04-20 | **Strong**: mature MLX ecosystem (`mlx-community/Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B-4bit` 13k+ downloads); ~1.19 GB bf16 | a: mod / b: good (below specialists) / c: good / d: **strong** (32k ctx, MRL) | Card discloses no training data/objective; no code benchmark of its own; used by NightOwl as miner (overlapping bias); larger vectors/latency | **High** availability, **medium** code-specific quality |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:68:- **S1 NightOwl-35M** is the direct drop-in replacement for BGE-small at the same footprint, with real code-retrieval evidence and an honestly self-disclosed 8% zero-shot fraction — the strongest signal of credible evaluation in the entire set.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:69:- **S2 CodeRankEmbed** adds an 8,192-token window, which is the key differentiator for "how does this work?" and cross-file queries that every 512/1,024-token model truncates.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:90:### Evaluation benchmarks (ranked)
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:95:| **CORE-Bench** (2026) | [paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11864v1) | 180K+ queries, 823k/52k/106k qrels across 3 levels; multi-positive (10–41 rel/query); L3 = broader-context retrieval (docs/tests/config/call sites) | Yes (most explicit of any resource) | per-level, per-language | 11 langs incl. PHP, TS, JS, Python (no HTML) | L1 embeds CSN/CoSQA; L2/L3 use widely-memorized SWE-bench repos → still upper-bound |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:96:| **CodeRAG-Bench** | [repo](https://github.com/code-rag-bench/code-rag-bench) | BEIR-format `corpus.jsonl`/`queries.jsonl`/`qrel/test.tsv` + execution-based end-to-end | Yes | NDCG/MRR/Recall/Precision @1–1000 | retrieval pool incl. Python, JS, TS, PHP, HTML | No license; unmaintained since Nov 2024; Java/Pyserini needed only for BM25 |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:102:- **HTML/templates: no labeled retrieval benchmark exists in anything fetched.** Only CoIR's CodeFeedback-ST language list includes HTML with qrels. If HTML/template retrieval matters, you must build your own eval set — raw material: [CommitPack html splits](https://huggingface.co/datasets/bigcode/commitpack), [The Stack `html`](https://huggingface.co/datasets/bigcode/the-stack), [code-rag-bench/github-repos](https://huggingface.co/datasets/code-rag-bench/github-repos).
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:113:| **Sentence Transformers** (trainer + losses + miner + evaluator) | [repo](https://github.com/UKPLab/sentence-transformers), [losses](https://sbert.net/docs/package_reference/sentence_transformer/losses.html), [miner](https://sbert.net/docs/package_reference/util/hard_negatives.html), [eval](https://sbert.net/docs/package_reference/sentence_transformer/evaluation.html), [model](https://www.sbert.net/docs/package_reference/sentence_transformer/model.html) | ST + PyTorch 2.2+ + HF datasets | **Yes** — MNRL = "InfoNCE / SimCSE / in-batch negatives" with `hardness_mode` | **Yes** — `mine_hard_negatives()` (range, margins, CrossEncoder rescoring, triplet/n-tuple output) | **Yes** — `InformationRetrievalEvaluator`: MRR, Recall@k, NDCG, MAP@k, precision@k; `NanoBEIREvaluator` | Yes — runnable end-to-end; local CSV/JSON or HF datasets | **`device="mps"`** supported | Apache-2.0 | **High** — only stack with all four pieces in one framework |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:114:| ST PEFT/LoRA on encoder | [example](https://sbert.net/examples/sentence_transformer/training/peft/README.html) | ST + PEFT | Yes (MNRL) | No | Partly (NanoBEIR NDCG@10: PEFT 0.4705 vs full 0.4728) | Medium — runnable `add_adapter`/`load_adapter`; script filename not on page; no hard-neg mining in recipe | fp32 recommended; adapter = 9.44 MB = 2.14% of base | Apache-2.0 | High that encoder-LoRA works in ST; **low** that a code-retrieval LoRA recipe exists |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:115:| FlagEmbedding (BAAI) | [repo](https://github.com/FlagOpen/FlagEmbedding), [embedder README](https://github.com/FlagOpen/FlagEmbedding/tree/master/examples/finetune/embedder) | FlagEmbedding + PyTorch + DeepSpeed + optional faiss | Loss names not stated on pages; kd `kl_div`/`m3_kd_loss` documented | **Yes** — `hn_mine.py` (`--range_for_sampling 2-200 --negative_number 15`); JSONL `{query,pos,neg,pos_scores,neg_scores,prompt,type}` | Yes (partial) — ndcg@10, recall@100 default; no MRR/MAP | Yes — `FlagEmbedding.finetune.embedder.encoder_only.base` trainer | n.a. (no macOS docs; CUDA/DeepSpeed-shaped) | MIT | Medium — use for the hard-neg mining + data contract even if you train with ST |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:117:| CodeRAG-Bench | [repo](https://github.com/code-rag-bench/code-rag-bench) | Pyserini (BM25) + ST (dense) | No (retrieval+gen eval) | No | **Yes** — NDCG/MRR/Recall/Precision @1–1000; BEIR format | Yes for retrieval eval | n.a. (needs OpenJDK 11 + Maven for BM25) | n.a. (null) | High for eval, medium overall |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:119:**Key pipeline finding:** Sentence Transformers is the only stack where `MultipleNegativesRankingLoss` (explicitly the InfoNCE loss), `mine_hard_negatives()`, `InformationRetrievalEvaluator` (MRR/Recall@k/NDCG/MAP), and `device="mps"` are all documented together. Use `CachedMultipleNegativesRankingLoss` (GradCache) with a small `mini_batch_size` to get large effective batches within unified memory on the Mac. **Honest gap:** the one concrete LoRA-on-an-encoder example is on GooAQ (QA), not code, and has no hard-negative mining — so a code-retrieval LoRA recipe must be assembled, not downloaded.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:130:### 6.1 Frozen benchmark of 150–300 real developer questions
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:132:Build a private, repo-grounded benchmark from your own repositories (not public corpora — this is the only way to escape the systemic CSN/Stack leakage documented above). Target ~250 questions stratified across the slices below. For each question, hand-label the gold file(s) and, where applicable, the gold symbol(s)/function(s). Include a held-out "no answer in corpus" slice (~10–15%) to measure false-positive behavior.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:134:**Benchmark hygiene:** the 250-question set is a *frozen test set* — it is never used for training, model selection, or threshold tuning. Carve a separate small dev split (~30 questions) for calibrating the no-answer confidence threshold, and report no-answer precision on the frozen test set using a threshold chosen only on dev.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:136:### 6.2 Evaluation slices
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:146:| Architecture | 20 | "how does auth middleware chain together" | Needs whole-file / multi-file context → favors 8k-context models (CodeRankEmbed, jina) |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:147:| Test/code linkage | 20 | "which test covers the refund path" | Tests ↔ implementation retrieval |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:156:| MRR@10 | First-relevant ranking quality | Standard IR metric, comparable to CoIR |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:159:| Latency (p50/p95) | End-to-end retrieval time per query | Apple Silicon budget constraint |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:165:0. **Apple Silicon smoke-test gate (required before any candidate is benchmarked):** run a one-batch encode and one training step on MPS for every model that requires `trust_remote_code` (CodeRankEmbed, CodeSage, jina, Qwen3). Some custom modeling code uses ops that MPS does not support; confirm `PYTORCH_ENABLE_MPS_FALLBACK=1` is set and that a train+save+reload round-trip succeeds before committing a candidate. This gate also applies to any fine-tuning of CodeRankEmbed — its training code is unreleased, so only fine-tune it after a smoke test confirms training/save/load works end-to-end.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:168:3. **Run all slices, compute all metrics.** Report per-slice, not just aggregate — aggregate CSN/CoIR numbers are unreliable upper bounds.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:180:| **(a) use an existing checkpoint as-is** | **Adopt for non-BGE checkpoints** | No usable BGE-small code checkpoint exists, but NightOwl-35M / CodeRankEmbed are credible as-is. Benchmark them first — you may not need to fine-tune at all. |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:184:| **(e) skip BGE Small, adopt a more code-capable embedding model** | **Choose this** | Evidence-based: code-native tokenizers, published per-language retrieval scores, hard-negative training, and 8k+ context are all available at the same or modestly larger footprint. |
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:188:1. **Benchmark phase (no training):** run the control group + S1 (NightOwl-35M) + S2 (CodeRankEmbed) + S4 (jina-v2-base-code) on your frozen 250-question benchmark. Use [CoIR](https://github.com/CoIR-team/coir) subsets as a fast regression harness and [CodeRAG-Bench](https://github.com/code-rag-bench/code-rag-bench) for the BEIR-format local eval.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:189:2. **If a code-capable base beats BGE-small + your Qwen control on NL→code slices** (likely): adopt it as the dense lane. For HTML/templates/config/schemas, keep a separate BM25 lane or use jina-v2-base-code (the only model covering those file types).
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:190:3. **Fine-tune only if the gap to your requirements is real** — and fine-tune *on top of the winning code base*, preferring **NightOwl-35M** (or its 150.8M sibling) as the ST-compatible encoder to fine-tune, since CodeRankEmbed's training code is unreleased. Use your own repo-derived (query, code) pairs with Sentence Transformers `MultipleNegativesRankingLoss` + `mine_hard_negatives()` + `InformationRetrievalEvaluator` on MPS, gated by the smoke test above. Supplement PHP with The Vault + CommitPackFT; supplement HTML/templates with CommitPackFT reformatted into query↔edit pairs.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:195:- If NightOwl-35M or CodeRankEmbed already hits **file-level Recall@10 ≥ 0.80** and **MRR@10 ≥ 0.70** on your Python/JS/PHP slices → **(a) use as-is**, spend effort on chunking + reranking instead.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/BGE Small vs Code-Capable Embeddings — Decision Report.md:196:- If it trails meaningfully on PHP or cross-language slices → **(c-adapted) fine-tune on your own repo-derived pairs on top of the code-capable base.**

./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-act1-plan-review.md:1:# RELAY · GH-11 Act 1 plan review — hybrid retrieval marathon
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-act1-plan-review.md:51:  `.embed-tmp/eval/score_retrieval.py`, `.embed-tmp/eval/queries-LTVera-Pandas.json`, `SOP.md`,
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-act1-plan-review.md:58:     `embed_repos.py`, metric definitions in `score_retrieval.py`, the query-prefix / `max_seq_length`
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-act1-plan-review.md:95:- [Blocker] **The promised exact metrics cannot be reproduced from the specified retrieval depth.** `.embed-tmp/eval/score_retrieval.py:133-155` ranks the entire corpus: a relevant result at rank 11 contributes 1/11 to MRR and is not `never_found`. p3:51-55 returns 10 hits from 50 candidates; p4:27-31,44-48 promises identical definitions without specifying eval depth. Its reference `per_query` row only contains query/relevant/rank/top_hit (`score_retrieval.py:147-148`), so it also cannot establish the top-10 reorder count requested at p4:67-68. **Fix:** explicitly define and test ranking depth per mode; either reproduce full-ranking metrics or call them MRR@10/miss@10 and rescore the baseline at that cutoff, recording the deviation. Add ordered chunk IDs/paths to per-query evidence and a rank-11 red case; preserve chunk ranks rather than deduplicating paths silently.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-act1-plan-review.md:103:- [Should] **The public retrieval API leaves mode and mismatch behavior underspecified.** p3:51 defaults mode to `hybrid` but p3:52-53 says a supplied reranker changes the default; it cannot distinguish an explicit hybrid request from that default. p3:56-57 refers to an RRF score even for dense/BM25-only modes, and a second embedder supplied to `Retriever` can differ from the one checked at Store.open (p2:55-56). **Fix:** use an explicit auto/None default, specify unsupported rerank and per-mode tau behavior plus empty results, and validate the retrieval embedder identity against the store. Test model, dim, and provider mismatches independently, including at query time; the current p2:74 test exercises model only.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-act1-plan-review.md:113:- [Blocker] Metric depth — **Implemented.** p4 now reports MRR@D / R@k / miss@D at an explicit depth D = 100 (`fetch_k=100` per lane) with its own dense-only arm at that depth as the baseline; Run 9 is context only. `per_query` rows carry the ordered `ranking`; ranks are chunk ranks (no silent path dedupe); rank-11 red case at depth 10 vs 100; `SearchResult.ranking` (p3) exposes the full candidate order. See brief-p4 §Context, Task 1, DoD; brief-p3 Task 5; plan §Recon.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-act1-plan-review.md:131:Swept the complete plan, MARATHON.yaml, all five briefs and validate.sh, including pre-existing content. Re-read the reference embedder, metric implementation, query labels, SOP and canonical Phase 0/1 plus the memory findings; fetched the live issue with `gh issue view 11 --repo HiQS-Labs/XYZ-code-intelligence --json body`. Graph `list_projects` returned all 50 entries with `has_more=false`; neither this worktree nor its source repo is indexed, so there is no applicable generation/coverage evidence. Used direct source inspection without indexing (review-only containment). No artifact/source execution, model probes, project gates or git commands. The findings below include unresolved and newly exposed defects in the swept files. Reversibility: Easy. Bet: fixing these bounded contracts before building avoids green checks that do not establish runtime readiness or retrieval correctness.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-act1-plan-review.md:138:- [Should] **BM25 threshold direction conflicts with the score contract.** p3:31 says native BM25 is lower-is-better, while p3:61-63 rejects a lane score *below* tau; unlike dense at p3:44, lexical at p3:41-42 never negates its score. **Fix:** define `Hit.score = -bm25(...)` (higher is better across lanes), or explicitly reverse the BM25 threshold comparison. Test stronger and weaker lexical hits around a threshold. Also qualify p3:74-75's “tau=None never does” assertion to nonempty candidates, since p3:63 correctly requires an empty set to return no-answer even without tau.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-act1-plan-review.md:155:- [Should] BM25 threshold direction — **Implemented.** Score contract is now stated once for every lane and stage: **higher is better**, with `bm25_search` returning `score = -bm25(chunks_fts)` alongside dense's `-distance`. Tests: a chunk matching two query terms scores above one matching one, and a tau between them keeps the stronger and rejects the weaker in `mode="bm25"`. The tau assertion is qualified as you asked: `tau=None` never yields no-answer **when at least one candidate exists**, while an empty candidate set is no-answer regardless of tau.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-act1-plan-review.md:169:- [Should] **The BM25 threshold test still contradicts the public threshold contract.** p3:75-78 defines a query-level decision using the top score: either all returned hits survive or the result becomes no-answer. p3:89-90 instead says a threshold between two candidate scores “keeps the stronger hit and rejects the weaker one”, implying per-hit filtering in the same result. **Fix:** preserve the specified query-level rule and make this two searches with controlled candidate sets/top scores on opposite sides of tau, explicitly asserting that a below-tau secondary hit remains when the top hit passes; or deliberately change the API to per-hit filtering and reconcile every mode. Keep the score-negation test separately.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-act1-plan-review.md:174:- [Pass] **Grounding, architecture, phase ordering and rating retain citable support.** Plan:60-70 agrees with `.embed-tmp/scripts/embed_repos.py:106,136-140,160-170` and `.embed-tmp/eval/score_retrieval.py:133-156`; p2:100-101 requires the sequence cap and query prefix from `FINDINGS-0.5.md:174-179` and `SOP.md:31-33`. Canonical:204-207,214-224 maps to p0–p4; plan:90-112 explicitly accounts for scope deviations without claiming the non-saturated win criterion complete. p2:51 names one writer; MARATHON.yaml's `depends_on` chain and `artifact` entries support the briefs; plan:150-161 assigns changelog ownership and Easy rollback. Plan:165-169 grounds 85/60/50/30, neutral appeal and no override. The historical user-order claim remains independently unverified.

./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-fire-or-review-161410/gh11-fire-or-review.codex.md:1476:    58	     `embed_repos.py`, metric definitions in `score_retrieval.py`, the query-prefix / `max_seq_length`
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-fire-or-review-161410/gh11-fire-or-review.codex.md:1513:    95	- [Blocker] **The promised exact metrics cannot be reproduced from the specified retrieval depth.** `.embed-tmp/eval/score_retrieval.py:133-155` ranks the entire corpus: a relevant result at rank 11 contributes 1/11 to MRR and is not `never_found`. p3:51-55 returns 10 hits from 50 candidates; p4:27-31,44-48 promises identical definitions without specifying eval depth. Its reference `per_query` row only contains query/relevant/rank/top_hit (`score_retrieval.py:147-148`), so it also cannot establish the top-10 reorder count requested at p4:67-68. **Fix:** explicitly define and test ranking depth per mode; either reproduce full-ranking metrics or call them MRR@10/miss@10 and rescore the baseline at that cutoff, recording the deviation. Add ordered chunk IDs/paths to per-query evidence and a rank-11 red case; preserve chunk ranks rather than deduplicating paths silently.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-fire-or-review-161410/gh11-fire-or-review.codex.md:1521:   103	- [Should] **The public retrieval API leaves mode and mismatch behavior underspecified.** p3:51 defaults mode to `hybrid` but p3:52-53 says a supplied reranker changes the default; it cannot distinguish an explicit hybrid request from that default. p3:56-57 refers to an RRF score even for dense/BM25-only modes, and a second embedder supplied to `Retriever` can differ from the one checked at Store.open (p2:55-56). **Fix:** use an explicit auto/None default, specify unsupported rerank and per-mode tau behavior plus empty results, and validate the retrieval embedder identity against the store. Test model, dim, and provider mismatches independently, including at query time; the current p2:74 test exercises model only.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-fire-or-review-161410/gh11-fire-or-review.codex.md:1531:   113	- [Blocker] Metric depth — **Implemented.** p4 now reports MRR@D / R@k / miss@D at an explicit depth D = 100 (`fetch_k=100` per lane) with its own dense-only arm at that depth as the baseline; Run 9 is context only. `per_query` rows carry the ordered `ranking`; ranks are chunk ranks (no silent path dedupe); rank-11 red case at depth 10 vs 100; `SearchResult.ranking` (p3) exposes the full candidate order. See brief-p4 §Context, Task 1, DoD; brief-p3 Task 5; plan §Recon.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-fire-or-review-161410/gh11-fire-or-review.codex.md:1549:   131	Swept the complete plan, MARATHON.yaml, all five briefs and validate.sh, including pre-existing content. Re-read the reference embedder, metric implementation, query labels, SOP and canonical Phase 0/1 plus the memory findings; fetched the live issue with `gh issue view 11 --repo HiQS-Labs/XYZ-code-intelligence --json body`. Graph `list_projects` returned all 50 entries with `has_more=false`; neither this worktree nor its source repo is indexed, so there is no applicable generation/coverage evidence. Used direct source inspection without indexing (review-only containment). No artifact/source execution, model probes, project gates or git commands. The findings below include unresolved and newly exposed defects in the swept files. Reversibility: Easy. Bet: fixing these bounded contracts before building avoids green checks that do not establish runtime readiness or retrieval correctness.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-fire-or-review-161410/gh11-fire-or-review.codex.md:1556:   138	- [Should] **BM25 threshold direction conflicts with the score contract.** p3:31 says native BM25 is lower-is-better, while p3:61-63 rejects a lane score *below* tau; unlike dense at p3:44, lexical at p3:41-42 never negates its score. **Fix:** define `Hit.score = -bm25(...)` (higher is better across lanes), or explicitly reverse the BM25 threshold comparison. Test stronger and weaker lexical hits around a threshold. Also qualify p3:74-75's “tau=None never does” assertion to nonempty candidates, since p3:63 correctly requires an empty set to return no-answer even without tau.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-fire-or-review-161410/gh11-fire-or-review.codex.md:1573:   155	- [Should] BM25 threshold direction — **Implemented.** Score contract is now stated once for every lane and stage: **higher is better**, with `bm25_search` returning `score = -bm25(chunks_fts)` alongside dense's `-distance`. Tests: a chunk matching two query terms scores above one matching one, and a tau between them keeps the stronger and rejects the weaker in `mode="bm25"`. The tau assertion is qualified as you asked: `tau=None` never yields no-answer **when at least one candidate exists**, while an empty candidate set is no-answer regardless of tau.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-fire-or-review-161410/gh11-fire-or-review.codex.md:1587:   169	- [Should] **The BM25 threshold test still contradicts the public threshold contract.** p3:75-78 defines a query-level decision using the top score: either all returned hits survive or the result becomes no-answer. p3:89-90 instead says a threshold between two candidate scores “keeps the stronger hit and rejects the weaker one”, implying per-hit filtering in the same result. **Fix:** preserve the specified query-level rule and make this two searches with controlled candidate sets/top scores on opposite sides of tau, explicitly asserting that a below-tau secondary hit remains when the top hit passes; or deliberately change the API to per-hit filtering and reconcile every mode. Keep the score-negation test separately.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh11-fire-or-review-161410/gh11-fire-or-review.codex.md:1592:   174	- [Pass] **Grounding, architecture, phase ordering and rating retain citable support.** Plan:60-70 agrees with `.embed-tmp/scripts/embed_repos.py:106,136-140,160-170` and `.embed-tmp/eval/score_retrieval.py:133-156`; p2:100-101 requires the sequence cap and query prefix from `FINDINGS-0.5.md:174-179` and `SOP.md:31-33`. Canonical:204-207,214-224 maps to p0–p4; plan:90-112 explicitly accounts for scope deviations without claiming the non-saturated win criterion complete. p2:51 names one writer; MARATHON.yaml's `depends_on` chain and `artifact` entries support the briefs; plan:150-161 assigns changelog ownership and Easy rollback. ./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Code Embedding Models — Full Evidence Table.md:19:**Scope of the question:** is it worth fine-tuning `BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5` locally (Apple Silicon) for retrieval over private repos containing Python, JS/TS, PHP, HTML/server-rendered templates, plus Markdown/tests/config/routes/API schemas — or is a different compact checkpoint a better starting point?
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Code Embedding Models — Full Evidence Table.md:27:**There is no credibly evaluated code-search fine-tune of `BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5`.** BAAI itself never shipped a small BGE code model: its only code embedding release is `BAAI/bge-code-v1`, which is not a BGE-small derivative at all — it is a 1.54B-parameter `Qwen2Model` (Qwen2.5-Coder class) with 1536-dim output and 32k context ([config.json](https://huggingface.co/BAAI/bge-code-v1/resolve/main/config.json), [repo metadata](https://huggingface.co/api/models/BAAI/bge-code-v1)). Querying the Hugging Face model index for every checkpoint declaring `base_model:finetune:BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5` returns 383 models, of which exactly two are code-related: `Matthieufromparis/bge-small-code-search-v1` and `ArnavKewalram/bge-small-code-v1` ([HF models API, filtered](https://huggingface.co/api/models?filter=base_model:finetune:BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5&limit=1000)). Both are weak evidence. The first was trained on only 6,000 CodeSearchNet Python pairs and reports NDCG@10 improving from 0.9761 (base) to 0.9849 on 500 held-out CodeSearchNet Python pairs — a saturated, single-language, self-built eval whose base-model score of 0.976 is itself implausibly high for real code search, indicating an easy candidate pool rather than a benchmark ([model card](https://huggingface.co/Matthieufromparis/bge-small-code-search-v1)). The second has **no license declared at all** and its card reports an "Unnamed Dataset" of 200,000 anchor/positive/negative triples with an `InformationRetrievalEvaluator` on a `cornstack_eval` split, but **publishes no evaluation numbers** ([model card](https://huggingface.co/ArnavKewalram/bge-small-code-v1), [repo metadata](https://huggingface.co/api/models/ArnavKewalram/bge-small-code-v1)). A BGE-large LoRA variant (`jamie8johnson/bge-large-v1.5-code-search`) is the only BGE-derived code fine-tune that reports a recognized benchmark — CoIR 9-task overall 57.5 vs. 55.7 for the BGE-large baseline, and CodeSearchNet (6 languages) NDCG@10 0.779 vs. 0.721 ([model card](https://huggingface.co/jamie8johnson/bge-large-v1.5-code-search)) — but it is a single-author repo with self-run evals and no paper.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Code Embedding Models — Full Evidence Table.md:29:**Why BGE-small is a poor base regardless of the fine-tune:** `bge-small-en-v1.5` is a 33.4M-parameter BERT with 384 dims, a **512-token limit**, English-only training on unnamed "large-scale pairs data," and no code benchmark of any kind reported on its card ([model card](https://huggingface.co/BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5), [config.json](https://huggingface.co/BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5/resolve/main/config.json)). Its tokenizer is the 30,522-token `bert-base-uncased` vocabulary, which is uncased and not code-aware — a structural handicap for exact symbol lookup (`getUserById`, `$wpdb`, `__init__`) that fine-tuning on your own data cannot undo.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Code Embedding Models — Full Evidence Table.md:31:**Better starting points exist at the same or smaller size.** The strongest finding of this research is `Shuu12121/NightOwl-CodeEmbedding-35M`: 34,094,976 params, 384 dims, 1,024-token context, ModernBERT with a **50,368-token custom code BPE**, trained with 15 mined hard negatives per anchor and reporting a full MTEB(Code, v1) sweep — macro average 0.65555 across 12 tasks, CodeSearchNetRetrieval 0.86228 with per-language breakdown (Python 0.8926, JS 0.7718, PHP 0.8676), and explicit decontamination against the CodeSearchNet test split ([model card](https://huggingface.co/Shuu12121/NightOwl-CodeEmbedding-35M), [repo metadata](https://huggingface.co/api/models/Shuu12121/NightOwl-CodeEmbedding-35M)). That is the same footprint class as BGE-small with real code-retrieval evidence attached. Its 150.8M sibling scores 0.70907 macro ([model card](https://huggingface.co/Shuu12121/NightOwl-CodeEmbedding)), and `nomic-ai/CodeRankEmbed` (136.7M, MIT, 8,192 context) reports CSN MRR 77.9 / CoIR NDCG@10 60.1 with ONNX ports available ([model card](https://huggingface.co/nomic-ai/CodeRankEmbed), [repo metadata](https://huggingface.co/api/models/nomic-ai/CodeRankEmbed)).

./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Code Embedding Models — Full Evidence Table.md:129:Suitability legend: **a** exact symbol lookup · **b** NL→code search · **c** semantic "how does this work?" · **d** cross-file / cross-language retrieval. Ratings are my assessment from the cited evidence.

./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Code Embedding Models — Full Evidence Table.md:175:1. **`Shuu12121/NightOwl-CodeEmbedding-35M`** — [card](https://huggingface.co/Shuu12121/NightOwl-CodeEmbedding-35M). The direct drop-in replacement for BGE-small: identical footprint class (34.1M params, 384 dims, 130 MiB FP32) and identical sentence-transformers ergonomics, but with a code-native 50,368-token BPE, 1,024-token context (2× BGE-small), 15 mined hard negatives per anchor, explicit CSN-test decontamination, and a complete MTEB(Code, v1) sweep at macro 0.65555 with per-language CSN numbers covering Python (0.8926), PHP (0.8676) and JS (0.7718). It even publishes its own weakness (8% zero-shot, in-domain caveat), which is the strongest signal of honest evaluation in this entire set. Best first move: benchmark it head-to-head against your current BGE-small index before spending any GPU time on fine-tuning.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Code Embedding Models — Full Evidence Table.md:176:2. **`nomic-ai/CodeRankEmbed`** — [card](https://huggingface.co/nomic-ai/CodeRankEmbed). 136.7M params, MIT, **8,192-token context**, CSN MRR 77.9 / CoIR 60.1, and community ONNX/int8 ports for CPU-cheap local serving. The 8k window is the key differentiator for "how does this work?" queries that need whole files or multi-function context, which every 512/1,024-token model truncates.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Code Embedding Models — Full Evidence Table.md:179:5. **`jinaai/jina-embeddings-v2-base-code`** — [card](https://huggingface.co/jinaai/jina-embeddings-v2-base-code). Apache-2.0, 161M, 8,192 context, and the **only** model whose declared language list explicitly includes HTML, CSS, Markdown, Shell, Dockerfile and SQL — directly addressing the template/config/schema part of your corpus that every other candidate ignores. Accept that its retrieval quality is mid-pack (third-party CoIR 58.4, CSN-PHP 0.5701) and that it publishes no eval itself.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Code Embedding Models — Full Evidence Table.md:182:**Bottom line on the original plan:** fine-tuning `bge-small-en-v1.5` starts from an English-text encoder with an uncased non-code tokenizer, a 512-token window, and no code evaluation whatsoever, and the only two existing code fine-tunes of it are a 6k-pair Python toy and an unlicensed checkpoint with no published numbers. The same local footprint is available from checkpoints already trained on code with hard negatives and published per-language retrieval scores. Fine-tune *on top of one of those* (NightOwl-35M or CodeRankEmbed) rather than on top of BGE-small, and add a separate strategy — jina-v2-base-code, or lexical/BM25 hybrid — for HTML/templates/config/schema files, which no code embedding model in this survey claims to cover.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Code Embedding Models — Full Evidence Table.md:191:- Raw README files were read via `https://huggingface.co/<id>/raw/main/README.md` for the benchmark tables that the rendered pages truncated (CodeSage NL2Code, CodeT5+ CodeXGLUE, OASIS CoSQA/AdvTest/CSN).
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Embedding Fine-Tuning Training Pipelines — Full Evidence Table.md:19:Scope: evidence table for someone fine-tuning **BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5** for code retrieval on an **Apple Silicon Mac**.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Embedding Fine-Tuning Training Pipelines — Full Evidence Table.md:26:**A reproducible pipeline does exist, but it has to be assembled from two or three parts, and one piece — LoRA on a small BERT encoder for code retrieval — has no ready-made end-to-end example.** The strongest single stack is Sentence Transformers: it documents `MultipleNegativesRankingLoss`, whose default configuration is explicitly called "the InfoNCE loss, SimCSE loss, cross-entropy loss with in-batch negatives or in-batch negatives loss" and which supports `hardness_mode` values `in_batch_negatives` / `hard_negatives` / `all_negatives` ([Sentence Transformers losses reference](https://sbert.net/docs/package_reference/sentence_transformer/losses.html)); it ships a first-class hard-negative miner, `mine_hard_negatives()`, with `range_min`/`range_max`, `max_score`, `absolute_margin`, `relative_margin`, optional `CrossEncoder` rescoring, `sampling_strategy` and `output_format` in `triplet` / `n-tuple` / `labeled-pair` / `labeled-list` ([mine_hard_negatives API](https://sbert.net/docs/package_reference/util/hard_negatives.html)); it ships retrieval evaluation with `InformationRetrievalEvaluator` reporting MRR, Recall@k, NDCG, accuracy@k, precision@k and MAP@k, plus `NanoBEIREvaluator` ([Sentence Transformers evaluation reference](https://sbert.net/docs/package_reference/sentence_transformer/evaluation.html)); and it has a **concrete PEFT/LoRA-on-an-encoder example** — a `LoraConfig(task_type=TaskType.FEATURE_EXTRACTION, r=64, lora_alpha=128, lora_dropout=0.1)` added to `sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2` via `model.add_adapter(...)`, trained on GooAQ with `MultipleNegativesRankingLoss`, scoring 0.4705 NDCG@10 on NanoBEIR versus 0.4728 for full fine-tuning, with an adapter of 9.44 MB = 2.14% of base weights ([Training with PEFT Adapters](https://sbert.net/examples/sentence_transformer/training/peft/README.html)). **Honest gaps:** (a) that LoRA example is on a QA dataset, not code, and the docs page does not name the runnable script file or show hard-negative mining in the same recipe, so the LoRA + hard-negatives + code-data combination must be composed by the user; (b) FlagEmbedding, the official BGE trainer, documents `use_lora`, `lora_rank`, `lora_alpha`, `target_modules`, `peft_model_path`, `save_merged_lora_model` — but its concrete LoRA configs are the decoder-only `bge-multilingual-gemma2` and `bge-en-icl` examples targeting `q_proj k_proj v_proj o_proj gate_proj down_proj up_proj`, i.e. **no published LoRA example for an encoder-only BGE such as bge-small** ([FlagEmbedding embedder fine-tuning README](https://github.com/FlagOpen/FlagEmbedding/tree/master/examples/finetune/embedder)); (c) no repository found in this session provides a Mac/MPS-specific training recipe for embedding models — Apple Silicon support is generic PyTorch MPS (macOS 12.3+, `torch.device("mps")`, training explicitly supported, backend still described by Apple as beta: [PyTorch MPS notes](https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/stable/notes/mps.html), [Apple Metal/PyTorch](https://developer.apple.com/metal/pytorch/)) with documented limitations: not all ops implemented (`PYTORCH_ENABLE_MPS_FALLBACK=1`), whole model must fit in unified memory, `bf16` requires macOS 14.0+, and the MPS graph cache grows per unique tensor shape so padded/variable-length batches can exhaust memory ([Transformers Apple Silicon guide](https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/en/perf_train_special)); (d) the code-retrieval side is mostly **data and evaluation**, not training: CoRNStack's own training/curation code is not released ("We plan to release the full training and dataset curation code soon!", [CoRNStack repo](https://github.com/gangiswag/cornstack)), and CoIR / CodeRAG-Bench / BEIR are evaluation harnesses only; the runnable code-search *training* scripts that do exist (CodeXGLUE `run.py`, UniXcoder `run.py`) train RoBERTa/CodeBERT/UniXcoder encoders with CUDA-shaped defaults and report MRR only.

./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Embedding Fine-Tuning Training Pipelines — Full Evidence Table.md:94:1. **Sentence Transformers (trainer + `MultipleNegativesRankingLoss` + `mine_hard_negatives()` + `InformationRetrievalEvaluator`)** — the only stack where all four required pieces are documented in one framework, with `device="mps"` supported, InfoNCE semantics stated explicitly, hard negatives with margin/score filtering, and a full IR metric set (MRR, Recall@k, NDCG, MAP@k, accuracy@k, precision@k) ([training overview](https://sbert.net/docs/sentence_transformer/training_overview.html), [losses](https://sbert.net/docs/package_reference/sentence_transformer/losses.html), [mine_hard_negatives](https://sbert.net/docs/package_reference/util/hard_negatives.html), [evaluation](https://sbert.net/docs/package_reference/sentence_transformer/evaluation.html), [model reference](https://www.sbert.net/docs/package_reference/sentence_transformer/model.html)). Use `CachedMultipleNegativesRankingLoss` with a small `mini_batch_size` to get large effective batches within unified memory ([losses](https://sbert.net/docs/package_reference/sentence_transformer/losses.html)).
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Embedding Fine-Tuning Training Pipelines — Full Evidence Table.md:96:3. **CoIR (+ its MTEB integration)** — the cheapest credible *code-retrieval* scorecard: `pip install coir-eval`, 10 code datasets in BEIR/MTEB schema, Sentence-Transformers model support; run a subset (e.g. `cosqa`, `codesearchnet`) as your held-out benchmark ([CoIR](https://github.com/CoIR-team/coir)). Remember to substitute BGE's own query instruction for the `"query: "` prefix in their example.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Embedding Fine-Tuning Training Pipelines — Full Evidence Table.md:97:4. **CodeRAG-Bench retrieval half** — for a *local codebase-RAG* benchmark: convert your repo into BEIR format (`corpus.jsonl`, `queries.jsonl`, `qrel/test.tsv` under `retrieval/datasets`), then use `eval_beir_sbert_canonical.py` to get NDCG/MRR/Recall/Precision at 1–1000; repository-level corpora (`repoeval_repo`, `swebench_repo`) are pre-built ([CodeRAG-Bench](https://github.com/code-rag-bench/code-rag-bench)). Caveat: no license, unmaintained since Nov 2024, Pyserini/Java needed only for BM25.
./PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/Embedding Fine-Tuning Training Pipelines — Full Evidence Table.md:106:- **leiluk1/CodeSearcher** — mentions PEFT benchmarking for CodeT5+ and a Streamlit `app.py`, but the repo page states no training script, loss, metric, data format, license, or commit date, so it fails the "runnable training scripts + clear data format" bar ([repo](https://github.com/leiluk1/CodeSearcher)).

Dense/BM25 correctly negate lower-is-better distances (`xyz/retrieve/dense.py:34`, `xyz/retrieve/lexical.py:55`); hybrid uses rank-only RRF (`xyz/retrieve/pipeline.py:84`, `xyz/retrieve/fuse.py:16-24`); rerank wholly reorders its 50-item head from model scores (`xyz/retrieve/pipeline.py:87-101`). On 30 already-saturated queries, lexical rank fusion and a general reranker over code can genuinely degrade MRR.
