   226	## Phase 2 — Frozen private benchmark
   227	
   228	> **Rescoped 2026-09-07** after the Act 1 measurement and a ponytail/debug-mantra audit
   229	> (`PARKED/2026-09-07-phases-2-5-ponytail-audit.md`). The original 250-question, 9-slice spec
   230	> assumed a corpus that did not exist and mis-diagnosed the problem as sample size.
   231	
   232	**The problem is saturation, not volume.** On the 30-query set, dense retrieval returns the right
   233	file in the top 3 for *every* query (R@3 = 1.000, MRR 0.9444). A ruler reading 100% cannot rank two
   234	retrievers, and 220 more questions of the same difficulty produce a longer ruler that still reads
   235	100%. Issue #8 already showed why most of these queries are easy: prefixing each chunk with its file
   236	path moved MRR 0.80 → 0.98, because the answers are carried by filenames
   237	(`"probe klaviyo api rate limits"` → `scripts/probe_klaviyo_rate_limits.py`). **Any question a
   238	filename answers cannot separate two retrievers.**
   239	
   240	So the target is **difficulty, not count**: roughly **60-80 questions selected to be hard**, frozen
   241	before any model selection.
   242	
   243	**Corpus (decided 2026-09-07: XYZ serves the PHP/WordPress repos too).** The four originally indexed
   244	repos are Python/JS only — across all of them: 1 `.php` file, 0 `.blade.php`, 0 `.twig`, 0 `.tsx`.
   245	PHP coverage therefore requires indexing the WordPress repos, which are real and git-backed:
   246	
   247	| repo | .php | .js |
   248	|---|---:|---:|
   249	| `universal-child-theme-oct-2024` | 47 | 21 |
   250	| `KISS-woo-order-monitoring-alerts` | 59 | 1 |
   251	| `LTVera-Pandas` | — | 390 |
   252	| `rebalanceOS` / `aegis-sleuth-slack-bot` / `XYZ-forge` | — | 459 |
   253	
   254	**Blade and Twig are dropped as slices.** They are Laravel/Symfony conventions and appear **zero**
   255	times in this stack; WordPress uses plain PHP templates. The template slice is retargeted to WP theme
   256	templates and HTML, which do exist.
   257	
   258	Slices, sized to the corpus (~70 questions):
   259	
   260	| slice | n | why it is hard |
   261	|---|---:|---|
   262	| behaviour-described, not named | 15 | the query shares no token with the file or symbol name |
   263	| cross-file / "how does X work" | 12 | the answer spans call sites, not one chunk |
   264	| PHP (WordPress plugin + theme) | 12 | new corpus; hooks/filters indirection |
   265	| Python | 10 | behaviour-led, not filename-led |
   266	| JS | 8 | behaviour-led |
   267	| WP templates + HTML | 6 | markup with logic embedded |
   268	| test ↔ implementation linkage | 5 | asymmetric naming |
   269	| no-answer negatives | 8 | must return nothing, not a plausible wrong chunk |
   270	
   271	A separate ~15-question dev split tunes τ. The frozen set is never used for training or selection.
   272	
   273	**Reuse, do not rebuild.** The query-file format (`.embed-tmp/eval/queries-*.json`) already works and
   274	`xyz eval` already computes MRR@D / R@k / miss@D with chunk-id-preserving rankings. `xyz/eval/metrics.py`
   275	is the **canonical** scorer; `.embed-tmp/eval/score_retrieval.py` and `ask_self_eval.py` should call it
   276	rather than reimplement the metrics — three implementations exist today and that is two too many.
   277	
   278	Metrics: file- and symbol-level Recall@5/@10, MRR@10, nDCG@10, no-answer precision, latency p50/p95.
   279	Indexing throughput and index size are already emitted by `xyz ingest`; no new instrumentation.
   280	Bootstrap confidence intervals; no winner declared inside overlapping CIs.
   281	
   282	**Dropped:** end-to-end agent context success rate (top-5 chunks fed to a coding agent, downstream
   283	pass rate). That is an agent-in-the-loop evaluation system — its own non-determinism, cost and
   284	flakiness — built to referee a difference the direct metrics cannot yet measure. Revisit only if two
   285	candidates tie on the harder set.
   286	
   287	**QA gate:** every question hand-verified; **at least one arm scores below R@3 = 1.000** (proof the
   288	set actually discriminates — a set that saturates again has failed its purpose); the PHP repos are
   289	indexed and their gold paths resolve; the harness runs unattended and emits one scorecard.

    20	## Finding 1 — Phase 4 is already excluded by Phase 3's own decision rule
    21	
    22	**Severity: high (deletes an entire phase).**
    23	
    24	Phase 3's rule, verbatim: *"if a candidate hits file-level Recall@10 ≥ 0.80 AND MRR@10 ≥ 0.70 on the
    25	Python, JS/TS, and PHP slices → adopt as-is and invest in chunking + reranking; **skip Phase 4**."*
    26	
    27	CodeRankEmbed measured **R@10 = 1.000** and **MRR = 0.9444** — clearing both bars by ~20 and ~24
    28	points. The rule fires "adopt as-is."
    29	
    30	Phase 4 is LoRA fine-tuning, triplet mining, benchmark deduplication and optional distillation. Its
    31	own preamble says *"Only if the Phase 3 rule fails."* It has not failed.
    32	
    33	**Honest caveat, stated because it cuts against the finding:** the current set is saturated, so these
    34	numbers are an upper bound and a harder set could drop the model below the bar. That is a reason to
    35	**defer Phase 4 until the harder set exists**, not a reason to build it now. Nothing about fine-tuning
    36	gets cheaper by starting early, and every hour spent there is unrecoverable if the rule holds.
    37	
    38	**Action:** mark Phase 4 *deferred — decision rule already satisfied on available evidence; re-open
    39	only if the Phase 2 replacement set drops CodeRankEmbed below R@10 0.80 / MRR 0.70.*
    40	
    41	---
    42	
    43	## Finding 2 — A third of Phase 2's benchmark targets languages the corpus does not contain
    44	
    45	**Severity: high (the spec cannot be executed as written).**
    46	
    47	Phase 2 specifies 250 questions in 9 slices, including **PHP 30**, **HTML/Blade/Twig 25**, and
    48	**cross-language PHP↔JS/TS bridging 30** — **85 of 250 (34%)**.
    49	
    50	File census across all four indexed repos (`find`, excluding `node_modules`/`.venv`/`.git`):
    51	
    52	| repo | .py | .js | .ts | .tsx | .php | .blade.php | .twig | .html |
    53	|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
    54	| LTVera-Pandas | 660 | 390 | 0 | 0 | **1** | 0 | 0 | 220 |
    55	| aegis-sleuth-slack-bot | 54 | 325 | 2 | 0 | **0** | 0 | 0 | 11 |
    56	| rebalanceOS | 550 | 21 | 8 | 0 | **0** | 0 | 0 | 6 |
    57	| XYZ-forge | 200 | 113 | 6 | 0 | **0** | 0 | 0 | 80 |
    58	| **total** | 1,464 | 849 | 16 | **0** | **1** | **0** | **0** | 317 |
    59	
    60	One PHP file. Zero Blade, zero Twig, zero TSX. You cannot hand-label 85 questions against content
    61	that is not there. TS is also thin (16 files) against a 30-question slice.
    62	
    63	This is **not** "the plan is wrong" — the operator demonstrably works in PHP/WordPress elsewhere
    64	(mu-plugins, Woo plugins, child themes are all in the working directories). The plan and the corpus
    65	simply disagree about which repos XYZ serves.
    66	
    67	**Action — pick one, do not split the difference:**
    68	- (a) Index the PHP/WordPress repos, and the PHP slices become real; or
    69	- (b) Drop the PHP/Blade/Twig/cross-language slices and size the set to the corpus we have.
    70	
    71	Until that is decided, Phase 2 cannot start on 34% of its scope.

    75	## Finding 3 — Phase 2 needs *harder* questions, not 250 of them
    76	
    77	**Severity: medium (8x scope reduction).**
    78	
    79	Diagnosis first (debug-mantra: know the fail path). The failure is **not** "30 is too few for
    80	statistical power." It is **saturation**: dense already returns the right file in the top 3 for
    81	**every single query**. A ruler that reads 100% cannot show that one arm beats another, and adding
    82	220 more questions of the same difficulty produces a longer ruler that still reads 100%.
    83	
    84	What actually restores discriminating power is difficulty: questions whose answer is *not*
    85	name-carried. The project already proved the mechanism — issue #8 showed that simply prefixing each
    86	chunk with its file path moved MRR 0.80 → 0.98, because most of these queries are answerable from
    87	filenames alone (`"probe klaviyo api rate limits"` → `scripts/probe_klaviyo_rate_limits.py`). Any
    88	question a filename answers cannot separate two retrievers.
    89	
    90	**Lazy version:** grow to ~60-80 questions **selected for difficulty** — behaviour-described-not-named,
    91	cross-file, "how does X work", and no-answer negatives — on repos that exist. Reuse
    92	`.embed-tmp/eval/queries-*.json` (the format already works) and `xyz eval` (already written, already
    93	depth-parameterised, already computes MRR/R@k/miss). Freeze it before the next model comparison.
    94	
    95	Skipped: the 250 target and the 9-slice matrix. Add when a real second corpus (PHP/WordPress) is
    96	indexed and the slices correspond to something.

     8	### GH-11 Act 1 — the XYZ retrieval library exists
     9	
    10	- Delivered canonical Phase 0 close-out + all of Phase 1 as a five-phase marathon (builder Codex,
    11	  reviewer agy); every phase reached `STATUS: Approved`. New `xyz` package: Tree-sitter chunkers
    12	  (Python, JS/TS/TSX, PHP) with path-prefixed chunk text and a widened walk, a SQLite store
    13	  (`chunks` + FTS5 + `vec0`) with a provider-keyed embed cache and a `WalkIncomplete` guard that
    14	  refuses to prune on a partial walk, a BM25 + dense -> RRF -> cross-encoder rerank query path using
    15	  sqlite-vec's `k = ?` form, and an `xyz ingest|query|eval` CLI. 45 tests; `validate.sh` green.
    16	- **Measured, and it cuts against the plan's premise:** at depth 100 on the 30-query labelled subset,
    17	  dense-only scored MRR 0.9444 / R@1 0.9000, hybrid 0.9222, hybrid+rerank 0.9016 — the reranked arm
    18	  is worst, and costs 50,004 ms p50 on CPU. It passed acceptance only because that was written as a
    19	  noise-floor test. The set is saturated (dense R@3 = 1.000) and cannot separate the arms.
    20	- Two defects were caught by the new `prelaunch.sh` readiness control *before* any builder turn ran:
    21	  `tree_sitter_language_pack` downloads grammars at first use (every offline turn would have failed),
    22	  and `cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2` returns `[nan, nan]` on torch 2.14 / transformers 5.16
    23	  while loading without error. Default reranker changed to `mixedbread-ai/mxbai-rerank-xsmall-v1`.
    24	
    25	Verification: `bash validate.sh` -> 45 passed, `validate: OK`; final Codex QA **VERDICT: PASS**
    26	(6 pass, 1 nit, 0 blockers), which also adjudicated the dense win as a property of the saturated
    27	benchmark rather than an implementation bug. PR #15.

ANSWER: Use a **50-question frozen test set**—the defensible TREC-style default. No better universal IR convention mandates another number. Make query type, not language, the primary stratification.

FINDINGS:

- [Pass] Allocate the 50 as: **24 behaviour-described single-target; 14 cross-file flow/architecture; 4 test→implementation linkage; 8 verified no-answer**. This directly replaces the current arbitrary ~70 allocation. The documented failure is difficulty, not sample size: filenames alone drove MRR 0.80→0.98. [Canonical build doc](/private/var/folders/69/3l_82qtj7fzglnt_jjg07jh40000gn/T/consult-wt-29148-6qid92db/PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/XYZ%20Code%20Intelligence%20v0.5%20%E2%80%94%20Canonical%20Research%20and%20Build%20Doc.md:232)

- [Blocker] Include **zero name-carried questions** in the frozen 50. For each of the 42 answerable items, reject it if a path-only retrieval baseline puts the gold file in its top 3. That makes filename exploitation unable to re-saturate the collection. Keep language as a recorded secondary quota: **Python 16, JS/TS 12, PHP 9, HTML/WP-template 5**. TS is folded into JS; Blade/Twig remain absent because the corpus has none. [Canonical build doc](/private/var/folders/69/3l_82qtj7fzglnt_jjg07jh40000gn/T/consult-wt-29148-6qid92db/PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/XYZ%20Code%20Intelligence%20v0.5%20%E2%80%94%20Canonical%20Research%20and%20Build%20Doc.md:243)

- [Should] Add **20 dev questions: 10 answerable, 10 no-answer** to tune τ. They must be disjoint from frozen questions **and gold chunks/files**; otherwise threshold tuning leaks selection information into the sole decision set. The repository already correctly states that frozen data must not be used for selection. [Canonical build doc](/private/var/folders/69/3l_82qtj7fzglnt_jjg07jh40000gn/T/consult-wt-29148-6qid92db/PROJECT/2-WORKING/v0.5/XYZ%20Code%20Intelligence%20v0.5%20%E2%80%94%20Canonical%20Research%20and%20Build%20Doc.md:271)

- [Should] Growth rule: **add 25 more hard questions if every compared arm scores answerable R@3 = 1.000 on the frozen 50.** That is a measurable recurrence of saturation, not a subjective concern.

RECOMMENDATION: Freeze **50 + 20 disjoint dev**, type-stratified and path-only-screened; do not preserve a filename-easy “representative” slice.
